It's just getting in the way of my work, so I figured I better come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, drugs, or medications—prescribed or otherwise—over these past few months?\n\nPatient: No, doc. I don't really drink, and definitely no drugs. You know, driving for a living, they test for that anyway, so you really can't mess around with it. And as for medications... no, I'm not taking any prescriptions or anything like that right now. Just my regular coffee to keep me going on the road, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few months, have you noticed any changes in your mood—like feeling unusually happy, energetic, or irritable for extended periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually have. For months now, it's like... I've been either way too fired up, just feeling like everything is gonna work out no matter what. Just real energetic and confident about it all. But then, on the other hand, I'm snapping over small stuff. Just little things at home or out working in the yard that normally wouldn't bother me. It just sets me off, which really isn't my usual way. I'm usually pretty level-headed.\n\nDoctor: When you say you've been feeling 'fired up' and energetic for months, has this feeling been pretty steady the whole time, or has it come and gone in distinct stretches—like maybe a few weeks of feeling that way, then it shifts to something else?\n\nPatient: It's been pretty steady, honestly. I mean, this whole thing has been going on for about seven months now. It started right around the time my daughter moved back home. I've just been trying to keep everything steady with her being back, and ever since then, I've just been stuck in this... fired-up mode. It really hasn't let up.\n\nDoctor: During these past seven months when you've been feeling fired up and energetic, have there been times when your mood shifted to feeling down, sad, or depressed—where things that normally interested you didn't seem worth doing anymore?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't had any times where I felt sad or down like that. It hasn't been up and down... it's just been that constant high energy and getting easily annoyed, like I mentioned. I haven't lost interest in my regular stuff or felt depressed at all. It's just been, you know, steady on the gas this whole time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this fired-up energy and irritability has been pretty steady for seven months. During that time, have you noticed any stretches—even just a few days or a week—where your mood or energy level dipped noticeably lower than your baseline, or has it truly stayed at that same high-energy level the entire time?\n\nPatient: No, it really hasn't dropped off at all. Just... steady. I haven't had any days or weeks where I felt lower than normal, or sluggish, or anything like that. Like I was saying, it's just been stuck in that same high gear the entire time.\n\nDoctor: During these seven months of feeling fired up and energetic, have you found yourself doing things on impulse—like making sudden purchases, taking risks, or acting without thinking—more than you normally would?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really done anything like that. I mean, I still just go to work, drive my routes, come home. I haven't been out spending money I don't have or taking any crazy risks. It's just the energy and feeling annoyed, not really doing anything wild or impulsive.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on something that requires your attention—like reading a delivery note or having a conversation—do you find it hard to stick with it even when you want to, or is it more that you're choosing to shift your attention to something else that seems more interesting in the moment?\n\nPatient: Well, it's more like I just can't stick with it, even if I try. That's actually what finally got me to come in here. Over these past few months, my mind just keeps jumping to every little thing. Just the usual routine, really.\n\nDoctor: Before these past seven months, when you were working as a driver, did you ever have problems with your attention jumping around like this, or was your focus pretty solid back then?\n\nPatient: No, my focus was pretty solid back then. It's just getting in the way of my work, so I figured I better come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, drugs, or medications—prescribed or otherwise—over these past few months?\n\nPatient: No, doc. I don't really drink, and definitely no drugs. You know, driving for a living, they test for that anyway, so you really can't mess around with it. And as for medications... no, I'm not taking any prescriptions or anything like that right now. Just my regular coffee to keep me going on the road, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few months, have you noticed any changes in your mood—like feeling unusually happy, energetic, or irritable for extended periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually have. For months now, it's like... I've been either way too fired up, just feeling like everything is gonna work out no matter what. Just real energetic and confident about it all. But then, on the other hand, I'm snapping over small stuff. Just little things at home or out working in the yard that normally wouldn't bother me. It just sets me off, which really isn't my usual way. I'm usually pretty level-headed.\n\nDoctor: When you say you've been feeling 'fired up' and energetic for months, has this feeling been pretty steady the whole time, or has it come and gone in distinct stretches—like maybe a few weeks of feeling that way, then it shifts to something else?\n\nPatient: It's been pretty steady, honestly. I mean, this whole thing has been going on for about seven months now. It started right around the time my daughter moved back home. I've just been trying to keep everything steady with her being back, and ever since then, I've just been stuck in this... fired-up mode. It really hasn't let up.\n\nDoctor: During these past seven months when you've been feeling fired up and energetic, have there been times when your mood shifted to feeling down, sad, or depressed—where things that normally interested you didn't seem worth doing anymore?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't had any times where I felt sad or down like that. It hasn't been up and down... it's just been that constant high energy and getting easily annoyed, like I mentioned. I haven't lost interest in my regular stuff or felt depressed at all. It's just been, you know, steady on the gas this whole time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this fired-up energy and irritability has been pretty steady for seven months. During that time, have you noticed any stretches—even just a few days or a week—where your mood or energy level dipped noticeably lower than your baseline, or has it truly stayed at that same high-energy level the entire time?\n\nPatient: No, it really hasn't dropped off at all. Just... steady. I haven't had any days or weeks where I felt lower than normal, or sluggish, or anything like that. Like I was saying, it's just been stuck in that same high gear the entire time.\n\nDoctor: During these seven months of feeling fired up and energetic, have you found yourself doing things on impulse—like making sudden purchases, taking risks, or acting without thinking—more than you normally would?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really done anything like that. I mean, I still just go to work, drive my routes, come home. I haven't been out spending money I don't have or taking any crazy risks. It's just the energy and feeling annoyed, not really doing anything wild or impulsive.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on something that requires your attention—like reading a delivery note or having a conversation—do you find it hard to stick with it even when you want to, or is it more that you're choosing to shift your attention to something else that seems more interesting in the moment?\n\nPatient: Well, it's more like I just can't stick with it, even if I try. That's actually what finally got me to come in here. Over these past few months, my mind just keeps jumping to every little thing. Like, I'll hear a CB chirp, or I'll see a fuel price sign out the window, or some new idea just pops into my head, and my attention is just... gone. I end up missing details that I normally never miss. I'll completely overlook a client's delivery note, or forget who I promised to call back. It's not that I'm choosing to look at something else, it's just that every little distraction pulls me away before I even realize it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble controlling urges to do things—like gambling, shopping, eating, or other repetitive behaviors—even when you knew they might cause problems? Or have there been times when you felt a strong urge to do something and just had to do it, even if you didn't really want to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't gamble, and I'm not much of a shopper. I eat pretty regular, mostly just whatever I can grab on the road. But no, I haven't had any strong urges to do things I couldn't stop, or repetitive habits like that. Just the usual routine, really.\n\nDoctor: Before these past seven months, when you were working as a driver, did you ever have problems with your attention jumping around like this, or was your focus pretty solid back then?\n\nPatient: No, my focus was pretty solid back then. I mean, driving a truck, you really have to pay attention to what you're doing and keep your eyes on the road. I never had any trouble staying on task or missing details before. It's really just been these last seven months or so that my mind started jumping around like this. Before that, everything was just normal.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these attention problems started about seven months ago when your daughter moved back home. Around that same time, did you have any head injuries, accidents, infections, illnesses, or any major changes in your health—anything like that?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. No accidents or... head injuries or anything. My health has been fine, really. I haven't been sick or had any major changes physically around that time. It's just been the same as always on that front.\n\nDoctor: Around the time your daughter moved back home seven months ago, did anything else change in your life—like your sleep schedule, your work routine, stress level, or anything else that felt different from your normal pattern?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my work routine was the same, just driving my routes. Sleep was... well, normal for me, I guess. The stress was maybe a little higher just because of having another person in the house, you know, adjusting to that, but nothing crazy. Everything else was pretty much exactly the same.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your health has been fine, but I want to ask more specifically: Have you had any headaches, dizziness, vision changes, memory problems beyond just attention jumping around, or any other neurological symptoms—like weakness, numbness, or coordination problems—at any point during these seven months?\n\nPatient: No, um... none of that. I haven't had any headaches or dizzy spells. My vision is fine—has to be for driving, you know? And no numbness or weakness or anything like that. My memory is alright too, I haven't been forgetting things. It's really just that focus issue where my mind bounces around. But physically, like with my coordination and all that, I'm completely fine.\n\nDoctor: In the few months before your daughter moved back home—so going back maybe six to nine months ago—did you have any serious infections, fevers, surgeries, or any medical events that required you to see a doctor or go to the hospital?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been in the hospital or had any surgeries or bad infections or anything. Like I said, physically, I've been completely fine. I haven't had to see a doctor for anything major before she moved back, or since.\n\nDoctor: Going back to when you were a kid or a teenager, did you ever have trouble paying attention in school, or did teachers ever say you were restless, fidgety, or had a hard time sitting still? Or when you were younger, did anyone ever mention that your mind seemed to jump around like it does now?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can remember. I mean, I did fine in school. I wasn't the best student or anything, but I didn't get in trouble for being fidgety or not paying attention. Nobody ever said anything about my mind jumping around back then. This whole feeling of my thoughts bouncing all over the place... that's really just been happening lately, not when I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Since these attention problems started seven months ago, has your regular doctor or anyone else ordered any tests on you—like blood work, an EKG, a CT scan, an MRI, or anything like that—to try to figure out what's going on?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of those tests. Since I haven't been to see my regular doctor for this, nobody has ordered any blood work or scans or anything. Like I said, I've just been dealing with it on my own, so there haven't been any medical tests done.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have thyroid problems, diabetes, or any other conditions with your hormones or metabolism—like your blood sugar or anything like that?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never had any issues with my thyroid or blood sugar, or anything like that. As far as I know, my metabolism and all that is perfectly fine. Never been diagnosed with diabetes either.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever mentioned to you that you might need to get your brain checked out—like with an MRI or CT scan—or have you ever wondered if there could be something physical going on in your head that might be causing these attention problems?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. And honestly, I haven't really thought about it being something physical in my brain, either. I just figured it was stress or, you know, whatever this is that's been going on with me lately. But no, no one's suggested I get my head checked or get any scans.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent changes in how you feel physically—like unusual fatigue, weight changes, trouble sleeping, or feeling hot or cold more than usual—anything like that in the past seven months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my weight's been about the same, and I haven't noticed feeling too hot or cold or anything like that. My sleeping is mostly normal for me, maybe a little off here and there because of the job, but nothing out of the ordinary. And physically, I feel okay, just... tired sometimes, but not like an unusual fatigue. Just regular tired. So, no, no big physical changes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

  "candidates": ["F90.9"],
  "note": "Ruled out F06.8 (Other specified mental disorders due to known physiological condition). Patient denies head injuries, infections, illnesses, neurological symptoms, medical events, thyroid problems, diabetes, and has had no medical workup. No evidence of medical etiology. The sustained 7-month presentation of inattention, hyperactivity/elevated energy, and irritability without mood episodes, impulsivity, or prior childhood symptoms is most consistent with Adult ADHD (F90.9), though atypical in onset. No organic medical cause identified after comprehensive screening.",
  "is_final": true
}
